The required thundercloud may be natural, or may be prepared by sending at least two B-rank or higher Katon techniques into open sky within the previous 100 IC. After Kirin resolves, the local electrical charge is spent; another Kirin cannot use the same setup.

Kirin cannot be parried by a technique below A-rank.

# **Suiton: Water Style**
{:#suiton-water-style}

Water (Suiton)

Water is at a disadvantage among elemental jutsu in that it requires raw material to use (earth techniques do as well, but it's rare to find oneself in a situation without ground).Unless the fight is taking place near a river (a small stream won't cut it) or a lake, or, say, on a boat, it's not going to be from the environment.

Accordingly, most water jutsu tend to be very weak or have limited effectiveness unless the shinobi supplies enough “Gallons of Water”, not to mention quite costly. However, using enough gallons of water can turn an ineffectual technique into quite a fearsome force. Even water, in enough quantity, can be turned into a destructive tsunami. 

Using “Gallons of Water” is quite easy, you simply mention how many Gallons you are investing into a Jutsu when you use it, and modify its “G” (Standing for, you guessed it, Water) variable according to how many Gallons you chose. Though it should be kept in mind that most Suiton Jutsus have a minimum and a maximum amount of Water you can invest into it (Typically displayed like this: 4 \> 12 Gallons of Water), anything more or less and the technique breaks down. If a Technique has a Gallon requirement of 0, that means you may use it without spending any Water on it, though that also means it’ll be quite weak.

Only one quarter of Gallons invested in a Suiton jutsu are consumed (rounded up). The remaining Gallons are deposited back onto the battlefield after the technique resolves. Minimum Gallon requirements are checked when the technique is used, before any Gallons are returned.

There are two primary ways to bring water into a battle where there otherwise is none: the first is to physically carry it, typically in a large gourd (see the Equipment chapter). The other is the D rank Water Creation Technique, which may add Gallons of Water to the Background, making those Gallons accessible to everybody in the battlefield until the Water is used up.

Used water is typically scattered across the battlefield, or evaporates back into the atmosphere as pure chakra, meaning it cannot be used in a technique again--it is lost. 

You can't use water someone else is carrying into battle (Like through a Container), but water in the Background or environment is fair game.

## **E-Rank**
{:#e-rank-9}

#### **Haneru \- Splash**
{:#haneru-splash}

A deliberately tiny Suiton exercise: the user flicks a controlled burst of water with just enough force to drench a person, cool a surface, or put out something that was never meant to be a battlefield inferno.

Chakra: 5  
Range: 3  
Area: 1  
Speed: 3  
Seal Speed: 2  
Tags: Utility, Projectile  
Effects: Create roughly 1 gallon of clean water at the target point. This deals no damage. It extinguishes ordinary non-chakra flame in its Area and may remove 1 point of Ignite from a willing target, but cannot affect Burns or an Ignite created by a B-Rank or higher technique.

#### **Mizudama no Jutsu \- Water Sphere Technique**
{:#mizudama-no-jutsu-water-sphere-technique}

The user gathers the water used in this technique in front of them as they perform the handseals, and then directs it at an opponent as a high-speed projectile.

Chakra: 5 \+ G\*2  
Damage: 3d10 \+ G\*8  
Range: 15  
Speed: 7  
Seal Speed: 5 \+ G  
Gallons Requirement: 0 \> 2

Tags: Attack, Projectile, Blunt

#### **Mizuhane \- Water Splash**
{:#mizuhane-water-splash}

Chakra: 5 \+ G\*2  
Speed: \+G  
Seal Speed: G\*3  
Gallons Requirement: 1 \> 90  
Tags: Link  
Effects: This is used alongside a Dodge Interrupt, and provides it with a \+G bonus. After your first time using this in a Combat Encounter, your enemies begin to see it coming. Each time you wish to use it, make an opposed Espionage vs their Espionage, with a \+5 for each time they’ve made this check this combat, on a success apply the effects as normal, on a failure you instead gain \+G/2 to your dodge. Each time you fail for this combat the divisor of this increases by 1 (G/2, G/3, G/4, G/5…) 

## **D-Rank**
{:#d-rank-9}

#### **Suiton: Mizu Doka \- Water Blending Technique**
{:#suiton-mizu-doka-water-blending-technique}

A thin film of chakra-laced water bends light around the user's outline and carries the colours of the surrounding terrain across their body. It is less perfect than true invisibility, but it works while moving and is especially effective in rain, mist, rivers, and wet terrain.

Upkeep: 2  
Speed: 8

Effects: While maintained, gain \+4 Stealth against checks that rely primarily on sight. If you are stationary in rain, mist, shallow water, or similarly wet terrain, this bonus increases to \+6.

This technique does not conceal sound, scent, footprints, or chakra. Making an attack or using a damaging Jutsu ends the Upkeep after that action resolves.

{:#mekkyaku-extinguishment}

Speed: 6

{:#mizu-souzou-no-jutsu-water-creation-technique}

You convert raw chakra into pure water. This technique is popular among serious users of suiton techniques, as it lets them avoid picking and choosing their battles so carefully, and allows them to eschew the practice of carrying around large gourds of water (which, in addition to giving away their specialty, are unpleasantly heavy).

Chakra: 5 \+ X  
Speed: 8  
Seal Speed: 5 \+ X  
Tags: Utility, Link

When used this way, add half X (rounded up) to that Jutsu's Chakra cost; Water Creation adds no Seal Speed and does not resolve as a separate action. It may also be used alone, in which case the created water is deposited on the battlefield or in a container you are wearing that has space for it.

#### **Suiton: Namerakana Amamizu \- Water Release: Rainwater Slick**
{:#suiton-namerakana-amamizu-water-release-rainwater-slick}

The user splashes a small area with water, infusing it with their chakra. This layer of chakra causes the water to support a person's weight for just long enough that when it gives out, they'll risk slipping and falling embarrassingly. Anything coated in this is, similarly, hard to hold on to.

Chakra: 12  
Range: 25  
Area: 15  
Speed: 8

Anyone who moves through this area by any means must choose to halve their speed or be forced to roll an Athletics check against that number. On a failure they become prone. This extends to determining penalties for AoE attacks. If they choose to move at full speed, they must make an Athletics Check against the same TN or fall Prone (though they still move the full amount). The Prone’s penalties do apply to any Defense against the Attack this was in response to.

Anybody inside the Area of Rainwater Slick suffers from Immobilization \-1, as they have to put a little more thought into all of their actions.

{:#suiton-shigure-water-release-drizzle}

Effects: You may create X whips to a max of Chakra Control Ranks /5. The range of all taijutsu attacks is increased by 5 \+ X, but used in this way converts the attack into ranged and it gains knockback equal to half the range.

## **C-Rank**
{:#c-rank-9}

#### **Mizu Bunshin no Jutsu \- Water Clone Technique**
{:#mizu-bunshin-no-jutsu-water-clone-technique}

An advanced form of the standard Bunshin no Jutsu, this creates clones out of water. Since they're solid (well, relatively) these clones can be used to perform tasks the user is unable or unwilling to do for themselves. While they're weaker than their user, they can still prove dangerous in combat.

Chakra: 10 \+ G  
Speed: 6 \+ 2\*X  
Seal Speed: 10 \+ X\*3  
Gallons Requirement: 5\*X

Tags: Utility, Clone  
Effects: You create X clones following all Bushin rules with the following exceptions:

A clone can be created with a Sword (From the Standard Equipment options) in hand by spending an additional 2 Gallons of Water on each clone. You may spend up to your Chakra Control \- 5 Artisan Points on upgrading these swords, splitting it between all of your clones. 

\[1 AP\] Dodge, or Parry.  
\[Variable AP\] Move: The clone may move around the battlefield. This costs 1 AP per 2 IC it spends moving.  
\[Variable AP\] The clone may make any basic unarmed or weapon attack, by paying 1 AP per 2 Speed of that action.  
\[5 \+ X AP\] The clone can use itself to create a water prison (as per Suiton: Suirou no Jutsu) and on a success the clone is destroyed. X is the number of gallons for the jutsu’s cost and requires no actual gallons. You must pay the Chakra cost rather than the clones (though they still gain the upkeep. If you go to the prison you can take it over by assuming it’s upkeep, and from then on you are treated as having used the jutsu.

#### **Mizugakure no Jutsu \- Hidden in Water Technique**
{:#mizugakure-no-jutsu-hidden-in-water-technique}

The user sinks into a shallow pool of water, much too little to actually fit a person (it's ninja magic, don't ask questions), where they'll be able to easily lie in wait for a target to pass by, or spy on someone who might be near.

Chakra: 18  
Upkeep: 3  
Speed: 14  
Seal Speed: 14  
Requires: 10 Gallons of Water on the field

Tags: Utility, Ground-Based, Aura  
Effects: You hide, and immediately enter Stealth 3\. If an opponent does not have a reason to suspect you're there (i.e. this isn't being done in-combat) you have a \+5 Stealth bonus. You maintain awareness of your surroundings outside of the pool as if it's a window.

The puddle being reduced below 10 gallons, results in you being harmlessly ejected.

#### **Mizutamari no Jutsu \- Pool of Water Technique**
{:#mizutamari-no-jutsu-pool-of-water-technique}

A moderately advanced technique is the appropriately named water-walking technique; molding chakra below one's feet to 'stand' on patches of water without any actual expenditure of energy, and, once it's perfected, only minimal concentration. This technique builds upon that concept, creating a 'cushion' of static, almost-solid water beneath each foot that the ninja stands on to avoid direct contact with the ground.

Chakra: 10 \+ G\*2  
Upkeep: 3  
Speed: 6  
Seal Speed: 14  
Gallons Requirement: 0 \> 6  
Requires: Water Walking Ability

Tags: Utility, Ground-Based  
Effects: You reduce any immobilization penalties due to the condition of the ground you're crossing by G, and ignore any other mechanical effects (such as damage for walking across caltrops) if they're C-rank or lower; if they're B-rank or above, you instead halve them. You are considered not directly in contact with the ground (for say, the Doton jutsu Shindou).

#### **Suiton: Mizuame Nabara \- Water Release: Syrup Capture Field**
{:#suiton-mizuame-nabara-water-release-syrup-capture-field}

The user converts chakra into water, and infuses it with enough chakra to alter its composition, making it extremely viscous. They then spit it out in a stream of sticky, highly adhesive liquid.

Chakra: 20  
Upkeep: 3  
Range: 25  
Area: 15  
Speed: 14  
Seal Speed: 16  
Tags: Utility, AoE, Environmental, Ground-Based  
Effects: If a person has the ability to walk on water, they may cross this area without difficulty. Anyone else in the area suffers a \-4 immobilization penalty.

This jutsu's effects persist for 50 IC even after you drop the upkeep. However, so long as the upkeep is maintained, you may control the syrup enough to have it try to 'grab' anyone using water-walking to cross it unaffected; if the attack hits, the person is stuck in the syrup as if they weren't using water-walking. This is a Speed 6 action, with a Chakra cost of 15\.

#### **Suiton: Mizurappa \- Water Release: Violent Water Wave**
{:#suiton-mizurappa-water-release-violent-water-wave}

Inhaling a large amount of air and performing the necessary seals, the user will expel a copious amount of water from his/her mouth. The wave of water produced will slam against its opponent and can then be utilized for other techniques.

Chakra: 19  
Damage: 10d10+60  
Range: 20  
Area: 15  
Speed: 12  
Seal Speed: 10  
Tags: Attack, AoE, Blunt  
Effects: This technique adds 5 gallons of water to the battlefield every time it's used.

#### **Suiton: Suijinheki \- Water Formation Pillar**
{:#suiton-suijinheki-water-formation-pillar}

A vertical wall of water rises to catch an incoming attack and collapse around it.

Chakra: 22  
Speed: 7  
Seal Speed: 12  
Tags: Interrupt, Barrier, Suiton  
Effects: 

Make a Ninjutsu Parry against a damaging Melee, Projectile, or AoE attack. On full success, protect only yourself unless the technique's Area explicitly fits behind the wall.

On success, add 10 gallons of water to your location after resolution. Against Raiton, suffer \-2 to the Parry.

#### **Suiton: Suikoudan \- Water Release: Water Shark Blast**
{:#suiton-suikoudan-water-release-water-shark-blast}

After creating the handseals, you launch water in projectile form at an opponent. Some ninja like to etch the outline of a shark's head into the end of this projectile; others, who believe that being a ninja is very serious business, prefer to omit that part of the technique. Experienced users can even use this to allow themselves to fly quickly through the water, while protecting themselves with the might of the tearing shark.

Chakra: 14 \+ G  
Damage: 10d12 \+ (G \* 7\) \+ 10  
Line: 3, G\*2  
Speed: 14  
Seal Speed: 12  
Gallons Requirement: 5 \> 12

Tags: Attack, AoE, Blunt  
Effects: You may move to any location along the area of this technique, unhindered by anything which would normally prevent you from moving. You may ignore other Ground-Based effects during this movement.

#### **Suiton: Suirou no Jutsu \- Water Release: Water Prison Technique**
{:#suiton-suirou-no-jutsu-water-release-water-prison-technique}

The user gathers the chakra around their hand, and forms the required water into a sphere which encloses its victim. Due to the density of the chakra used in this technique, the water is stronger than steel. It is not, however, airtight. Yeah, don't ask how that works. We don't know either.

Chakra: 15 \+ G  
Upkeep: 4  
Speed: 12  
Seal Speed: 16  
Accuracy: \-2  
Gallons Requirement: 5 \> 12  
Requires: 12 Chakra Control

Tags: Attack, Melee  
Effects: If this Jutsu hits, the victim is trapped inside a sphere of water. People inside cannot take actions, but they also cannot be affected by anything outside the prison; yes, that's right, the water can even stop giant swords. It makes sense, honestly, we promise, somehow.

Anyone with a Chakra Control Rank of G\*2 or above is able to mold the chakra around them to allow themselves to siphon air through the prison, meaning they can still talk and breathe. Any target with less CC, however, suffers a Suffocation 1 status every 10 IC. 

To maintain the water prison, you must keep one hand on it. Dodging requires you to end the effect; if you take damage, you must make a Chakra Control check against a TN of 5+(damage taken)/10 or the prison ends. Although you can't move, you can still take most other actions that are possible one-handed while maintaining the prison's upkeep.

#### **Umi no Tate no Jutsu \- Ocean Shield Technique**
{:#umi-no-tate-no-jutsu-ocean-shield-technique}

A derivative of a more advanced technique, this was created to help teach up-and-coming suiton users the foundations of that jutsu, and help keep them alive until they were skilled enough to learn it. It creates a circular barrier of water around you, which surges up to protect you from... well, whatever convinces you that you'd need to use this technique.

Chakra: 6 \+ G\*1.5  
Speed: 8  
Seal Speed: 8  
Gallons Requirement: 6 \> 12

Tags: Interrupt  
Effects: You parry an incoming attack with a Bonus of \+(G/3). This works against jutsu weak to it up to A rank, jutsu strong against it up to C-rank, and all other Jutsu (including Taijutsu) up to B-rank.

## **B-Rank**
{:#b-rank-9}

#### **Benzaiten no Jutsu \- River Goddess Technique**
{:#benzaiten-no-jutsu-river-goddess-technique}

Named after the deity who supposedly watches over a major river, this technique coats the user's body with a thin layer of chakra-infused water. It offers no real protection from impacts or weapons, but can be an effective supplement to one's defenses against heat and other unpleasantness.

Chakra: 5 \+ G  
Upkeep: 4  
Speed: 10

Tags: Utility, Self, Aura

#### **Suiton: Bakusui Shouha \- Water Release: Exploding Water Shockwave**
{:#suiton-bakusui-shouha-water-release-exploding-water-shockwave}

The user creates an enormous amount of water by spewing it from their mouth, flooding the battlefield and potentially crushing foes in its path. Best of all, they get to look awesome while doing it\! The wave rises up from beneath the user, and they can 'ride' it to its destination.

Chakra: 33  
Damage: 18d12+90  
Area: X\*3  
Speed: 17

Tags: Attack, AoE, Blunt

As part of this technique, you may move up to half its Area in yards, in any direction. The Area of this Technique does not move with you \- it remains centered on where your movement began.

#### **Suiton: Goshokuzame \- Five Feeding Sharks**
{:#suiton-goshokuzame-five-feeding-sharks}

Five chakra sharks move through water as a coordinated hunting pattern.

Range: 30

Seal Speed: 20  
Tags: Attack, Projectile, Suiton  
Effects: 

Requires the target to be touching a body of wat	er containing at least 20 gallons. Make up to five attacks; divide the five damage groups among targets in the same connected water.

A target uses one Defensive Interrupt but rolls separately against each shark. Combine damage only for Wounding after all hits.

#### **Suiton: Suidanha \- Water Severing Wave**
{:#suiton-suidanha-water-severing-wave}

A thin, high-pressure stream turns water into a cutting tool.

Chakra:Damage: 32d10+10  
Accuracy: \+1  
Range: 20

Tags: Attack, Projectile, Slashing, Suiton  
Effects: 

Requires 8 gallons of available water or \+5 Chakra to create it.

Against unattended objects and created Barriers, treat Damage as 25% higher. It does not bypass Armor or DR against creatures.

#### **Suiton: Suigadan \- Water Release: Water Fang Bullet**
{:#suiton-suigadan-water-release-water-fang-bullet}

This move forms multiple pressurized, spiraling columns of water that have a drill-like ability to pierce and destroy their target. As this attack can originate from any body of water (rather than being shot directly from the attacker), in the right environments this has a 360 degree range of attack, making it incredibly difficult to defend against adequately.

Chakra: 23 \+ G/2  
Damage: 14d10 \+ G\*5 \+ 10  
Accuracy: \+2  
Range: 35  
Speed: 15  
Seal Speed: 18

Tags: Attack, Projectile, Piercing

#### **Suiton: Suijinheki \- Water Release: Water Encampment Wall**
{:#suiton-suijinheki-water-release-water-encampment-wall}

This defensive technique creates a wall of water around you, which forms a violently churning defensive perimeter. When lacking water in the environment to use, a talented shinobi can form it in their stomach and spit it out.

Chakra: 26  
Upkeep: 4  
Area: 5  
Speed: 12  
Seal Speed: 16  
Requires: 16 gallons of water or \+8 chakra cost  
Tags: Interrupt, Environmental  
Effects: If using Suijinheki as an interrupt: Roll a parry against the attack's accuracy, with a \+6 bonus; if you succeed, this stops the attack entirely.

Once Suijinheki has been deployed, it lasts for your damage bonus in initiative counts--it automatically defends all attacks using your initial parry roll. It ends early if an attack breaks through the barrier or you may also end it early, if you need to move or attack through it.

This technique protects you and anyone within its area from any jutsu strong against this up to B-rank, and all other techniques (including taijutsu) up to A-rank.

#### **Suiton: Suikyō \- Water Mirror**
{:#suiton-suikyo-water-mirror}

Tags: Interrupt, Barrier, Suiton

Use against a Projectile Ninjutsu up to B-rank. Make a Ninjutsu Parry at \-2.

On success the attack is negated and the mirror breaks. If you beat the attack by 5+, the attacker suffers NDB×5 damage of the original technique's damage type; this reflected damage cannot Wound.

#### **Suiton: Suiryuudan \- Water Release: Water Dragon Blast**
{:#suiton-suiryuudan-water-release-water-dragon-blast}

This technique shapes a large amount of water into a giant, dragon-shaped projectile which seeks out and crashes into whoever you direct it at. What it lacks for in complexity, it makes up for by being a highly effective tool in any Suiton using shinobi's arsenal.

Chakra: 16 \+ G  
Damage: 20d6 \+ G\*10 \+ 15

Speed: 18

#### **Suiton: Takitsubo no Jutsu: Water Release: Waterfall Basin Technique**
{:#suiton-takitsubo-no-jutsu-water-release-waterfall-basin-technique}

This technique drills a hole with existing water to an underground source of water and draws it forth, forming a geyser on the surface. The issue, however, as you may have guessed, is that you already need to have water on hand to make that hole, quite a bit in fact. Rocks tend to get quite tough to dig through once you reach the "Hidden sources of water" level. Gotta spend water to make water, just the way the world works, sorry. Aside from being useful for creating a spot to relax when resting or camping out, and ensuring you'll always have a source of clean water, it, like most ninjutsu, has combat applications. 

Chakra: 22  
Speed: 10  
Seal Speed: 20  
Gallons Requirement: 10 \> 25

Tags: Utility, Environmental  
Effects: This Technique adds (G/5) Gallons of Water onto the Background every 5 IC, for a Duration of 100 ICs.

#### **Suiton: Uramiame \- Water Release: Grudge Rain**
{:#suiton-uramiame-water-release-grudge-rain}

As a weather-manipulation jutsu, this technique is extremely taxing upon its user. Upon its completion a large storm cloud is created, which proceeds to, as storm clouds are prone to do, rain. This rain saps the chakra from anyone it comes in contact with, and transfers it through the pooling water to the technique's user.

Chakra: 30  
Upkeep: 4  
Area: 30  
Speed: 14  
Seal Speed: 24

Tags: Utility, AoE, Environmental  
Effects: Every 10 IC, everyone other than the user and up to Chakra Control/5 people they've designated as allies has their Chakra Exhaustion penalty increased by 1, and the user has their Chakra Exhaustion penalty decreased by 1 per person present affected by the rain. If the user moves, they may cause the mist to 'follow' them; It moves at a speed of 0.5 Yards per IC until the user is back in its center.

## **A-Rank**
{:#a-rank-9}

#### **Dai Bakusui Shouha \- Great Exploding Water Shockwave**
{:#dai-bakusui-shouha-great-exploding-water-shockwave}

A stronger but less directly offensive version of the B-rank Exploding Water Shockwave, the user spits out a truly prodigious amount of water.

Chakra: 40  
Upkeep: 5  
Damage: 20d10+190  
Area: 80  
Speed: 22  
Seal Speed: 25  
Special: This technique receives only half your damage bonus.

Tags: Attack, AoE, Blunt, Environmental  
Effects: This adds 40 Gallons of Water to the Background. So long as there are at least 30 Gallons in the Background at any time while this Technique's Upkeep is maintained, any Ground-Based Techniques (Such as Earth Split Turnaround Palm or Syrup Capture Field) become impossible to use. Regardless, all Suiton Techniques treat the maximum of their Gallons Requirement as if it was 5 higher.

Additionally, anyone who is standing in its Area will begin sinking even if they have the Water Walking Ability (The currents are simply too tumultuous). They can swim at half their normal movement speed (movement-increasing jutsu typically will not work), and until they've exited the area suffer Suffocation 1 every 15 IC. Alternatively, they can take a Speed 2 action to stay at the surface for the next 15 IC.

#### **Suiton: Daibakufu \- Water Release: Great Waterfall**
{:#suiton-daibakufu-water-release-great-waterfall}

This is one of the most powerful water ninjutsu in existence; once the appropriate seals are formed, the user unleashes a devastating tidal wave which tears across the battlefield like a scaled-down tsunami, knocking aside people, trees, and small buildings in its path.

Long ago, exceptionally skilled shinobi were able to fill their impromptu waterfall with all manner of weapons, which were propelled within the wave at incredible speeds. This had the effect of making it an almost inevitably deadly technique that would leave pools of blood mixed in with the water. However, use of that aspect of the technique was forbidden due to its unbelievable power, and as peace settled across the lands the secret of how to do that was lost.

Chakra: 30 \+ G/2  
Damage: G\*10 \+ 30  
Line: 20, 80  
Speed: 24  
Seal Speed: 28  
Special: Stun 14  
Gallons Requirement: 20 \> 40

Tags: Attack, AoE, Blunt  
Effects: This Technique pushes any targets hit with a Knockback of G.

#### **Suiton: Daikoudan \- Water Release: Great Shark Blast**
{:#suiton-daikoudan-water-release-great-shark-blast}

This creates an enormous shark out of water and sends it crashing across the battlefield at whichever party has offended the user sufficiently for them to feel the need to do so. Aside from impacting its primary target, the shark is massive enough to cause significant collateral damage. The technique's true secret, however, is its ability to absorb any chakra it comes in contact with and use that energy to further empower itself.

Chakra: 30 \+ G/2  
Damage: 18d20 \+ G\*8 \+ 20  
Line: 5, 60  
Speed: 20  
Seal Speed: 26  
Gallons Requirement: 12 \> 30

Tags: Attack, Interrupt, Projectile, AoE, Blunt  
Effects: As an interrupt this technique's Seal Speed is reduced by 10, its Chakra cost is increased by 10 and can only target the attacker.

Daikoudan may be used to Interrupt techniques so long as its Speed is equal to or less than theirs, but can only Interrupt Projectile Attacks. Compare the damage of these attacks with Jutsu weak to it reduced by 25%, and Jutsu strong against it increased by 25%. If the incoming attack does more damage, you are hit by it, but it has its damage reduced by your damage. If Daikoudan's damage is greater, it becomes an attack against the opponent with any Accuracy bonuses (but not penalties) of the attack it countered.

Similarly, if a ninjutsu-based defense is used against Daikoudan, and fails to stop it completely, it does full damage (bypassing even partial defense).

#### **Suiton: Suiryuuben \- Water Release: Water Hydra Whip**
{:#suiton-suiryuuben-water-release-water-hydra-whip}

This jutsu creates a levitating orb of water which can project numerous whips and tendrils of water. These tendrils can be guided to targets by remote manipulation of the orb's chakra, even changing direction in mid-trajectory to find their targets. On top of that, they're sharp-tipped and more than capable of perforating opponents many times over.

Chakra: 25 \+ G/5  
Upkeep: 6  
Damage: 10d10+50  
Range: 40 \+ 5\*Ndb  
Speed: 20  
Seal Speed: 28  
Gallons Requirement: 50 \> 100

Tags: Utility, Environmental  
Effects: When you first use this Technique, you create a levitating ball of water, and perform one Tendral Whipping Attack (mentioned below) for free. A So long as you maintain this technique, your pet giant levitating ball of water will fight for you\! It acts 1 IC after it's created and can take the following Actions, by spending your AP:

\[X AP, Speed X\] Move: It moves as if it had taken a Speed (X)\*5 action, using your CHA in place of its AGI. It cannot (deliberately) move outside its max range. If it does end up outside that range, it can take no actions other than moving towards you.

\[3 \+ X\*3 AP, Speed 12, Piercing\] Tendril Whipping: You target X people within range. Your water ball makes a Projectile Attack against anyone within half this technique's max range, doing the above listed damage if it hits. This does piercing damage.

Additionally, the levitating water ball receives its own pool of AP equal to (G/2) which it may spend to perform the Action mentioned above and/or lower their own Speed.

This technique uses your base Accuracy at Fatigue 0 and half your NDB. Because the attacks are made by the technique, and not you, you cannot modify them further such as through Willpower or your own AP.

# **Bukijutsu**
{:#bukijutsu-2}

Bukijutsu builds on normal weapon attacks rather than replacing them. A Basic Weapon Attack remains the efficient, reliable option; Bukijutsu gives a weapon specialist stronger ways to exploit positioning, repeated defenses, reach, terrain, movement, and other openings. The core Chain structure is unchanged, but it is slightly more forgiving so using a sensible weapon action does not immediately erase the sequence you have built.

## **Learning Requirements**
{:#learning-requirements-2}

E-Rank Bukijutsu is universally available to player characters.

E-Rank — Athletics \-; Research \-.

D-Rank — Athletics 4; Research 5\.

C-Rank — Athletics 10; Research 12\.

B-Rank — Athletics 20; Research 22\.

A-Rank — Athletics 30; Research 32\.

B-Rank and A-Rank use the normal mentor rule. You must be taught the technique unless you exceed the relevant learning requirement by 50%, following the general Jutsu rules.

## **Buki Attacks**
{:#buki-attacks-2}

Unless a technique lists fixed Damage or Speed, an attacking Bukijutsu starts from the Basic Weapon Attack of the weapon being used and applies the modifiers printed in the technique. “dS” uses that weapon’s base damage die size.

A weapon attack may only be modified or replaced by one Bukijutsu unless another technique explicitly has the Link tag. Chain tags are not Link tags.

If your weapon has an innate delay, any Bukijutsu you cast gains a Delay equal to half your weapon’s delay.

If a Bukijutsu calls for an attack roll, that means a 1d20 \+ your Accuracy \- 10\.

When a technique refers to the same Defense, it means the same broad defensive action: Dodge, Parry, Block, Take the Hit, and so on. Different named Parry techniques are still Parry unless a technique specifically says otherwise.

Bukijutsu use Move with an Attack normally unless a technique says otherwise. If a technique lets you use Move with an Attack in another direction, calculate the distance normally from half the Attack's final Speed. It changes the direction or timing of that movement; it does not create a second movement allowance. If you already spent some of your Move with an Attack distance before the strike, only the remainder is available afterward.

## **Specials and Status Rolls**
{:#specials-and-status-rolls}

Bukijutsu use the normal Special and Status Roll rules. If a Bukijutsu lists a status in its Special line, use Athletics for the Status Roll unless the technique says otherwise; the target rolls Resistance normally. Stun follows its normal rules and is not separately resisted.

A Special attached to a damaging Attack only applies if the Attack still produces an effect after defenses and damage reduction, following the normal rules for statuses. Partial Success reduces a numeric Special in the same way it reduces the rest of the Attack's effects unless the technique says otherwise.

Knockback: Bukijutsu that list Knockback are allowed to hit harder when the user wins the Status Roll decisively. After a successful Athletics vs Resistance Status Roll, increase the listed Knockback by 1 yard for every full 3 points the target failed by, to a maximum of double the listed Knockback. Apply Partial Success to the resulting Knockback normally.

If a technique changes the direction of Knockback, it will say so. This still counts as Knockback and forced movement; it is not a separate Pull or Shift status.

## **Chain**
{:#chain-2}

Chain Value: A Chain is tracked against one target and has a value of 0, 1 or 2\. It has no passive damage bonus.

Chain 1 — Entry: May be used at Chain 0 or 1\. On a hit, establish Chain 1 against that target. A successful Chain 1 during an existing Chain keeps the value at 1\.

Chain 2 — Pressure: Requires Chain 1 or 2 against that target. On a hit, set the Chain to 2\. Further Chain 2 techniques may be used while at 2 and keep the value at 2\.

Chain 3 — Finisher: Requires Chain 2 against that target. The Chain ends after the technique resolves, hit or miss.

Chain X — Utility: May be used at any Chain value, including 0\. It never increases or decreases Chain value.

Chain Window: A Chain ends if 20 IC pass without you using a Chain-tagged Bukijutsu or making a Basic Weapon Attack against the chained target. A Basic Weapon Attack refreshes the window but does not change Chain value. Chain X refreshes this window unless its own text says otherwise.

Breaking a Chain: The Chain ends if you Wait, fully miss a Chain 2, become Stunned, lose access to the required weapon, use a non-Chain Attack against another target that is not Chain 1, or use a non-Chain non-Interrupt action other than a Basic Weapon Attack against the chained target. Ordinary movement, purely defensive Interrupts, and Bukijutsu Utility actions compatible with your weapon do not break Chain; unless they are Chain X, they do not refresh the Chain Window.

No Repeat Acceleration: A Chain 2 never gains a stacking Speed reduction merely because a Chain 2 was used before it. Technique-specific repeat bonuses cannot reduce Speed unless they explicitly say so, and no repeat bonus may stack with itself.

Repeat Debuffs: A temporary Accuracy, Defense, Parry, Dodge or similar numerical penalty caused by the same named Bukijutsu does not stack with itself unless the technique explicitly says otherwise. A new application refreshes or replaces the old one.

Changing Weapons: Chain value belongs to the target, not the weapon. Changing or readying weapons must cost Speed 0 or be handled by Chain X; otherwise the equipment change breaks Chain.

Cross-Class Finishers: To use a Chain 3 from a weapon class different from the class that began the Chain, at least one successful Chain 1 or Chain 2 since the Chain began must have used a weapon compatible with that Chain 3’s class. Dual counts as compatible only with the specific weapons actually used by that Dual attack.

Finisher Weapon: A Chain 3 uses only the properties of the weapon used for that Chain 3\. Damage, dual-hit percentages, wound riders, reach changes and similar benefits from earlier attacks do not carry into it unless the Chain 3 explicitly says they do.

### **Fan Bridge**
{:#fan-bridge-2}

Fan Bukijutsu has a deliberate exception to the normal Chain break rule. Seamless Wind may carry one Fan-tagged Fuuton through an active Chain. The Fuuton remains Ninjutsu, never becomes Chain 1/2/3, receives no Bukijutsu damage or status riders, and does not refresh the Chain Window. But your Chain does not break. You can do this once per Chain.

## **Bukijutsu Stances**
{:#bukijutsu-stances}

Stances are ordinary Self Upkeep Bukijutsu. You may maintain only one Bukijutsu Stance at a time; activating another ends the first. They do not end merely because you attack, move or defend. They use the normal Upkeep rules, may be activated through Preparation when legal, and interact with generic Upkeep effects only when those effects actually apply to Bukijutsu or to Upkeep generally. Effects written specifically for Ninjutsu Upkeep do not apply.

Bukijutsu Stances do not cost XP to learn and are automatically unlocked when you meet the relevant Bukijutsu rank requirements.

Bukijutsu Stances cannot be chosen as Signature Moves unless another rule explicitly allows it.

#### **High Guard**
{:#high-guard}

You hold the weapon high enough to threaten the first line immediately, accepting a more exposed defensive posture.

Rank: D  
Stamina: 8

#### **Low Guard**
{:#low-guard}

The weapon stays low and close to the body, making it easier to receive an attack before committing to your own.

{:#long-guard}

You extend the weapon into the space in front of you and make the opponent fight through its reach before they can reach your body.

#### **Open Guard**
{:#open-guard}

You deliberately leave a line available, using the invitation to make the defender commit before your real angle is chosen.

Stamina: 12

#### **Point Guard**
{:#point-guard}

You keep the point or striking line fixed on one part of the opponent and sacrifice broader coverage for precision.

Rank: C  
Stamina: 12  
Upkeep: 4  
Speed: 8  
Tags: Utility, Self, Upkeep, Bukijutsu Stance  
Effects: Reduce Called Shot Accuracy penalties with weapon attacks by 2\. Weapon attacks that are not Called Shots take \-1 Accuracy.

#### **Committed Guard**
{:#committed-guard}

You build every movement around the next heavy commitment, gaining force at the cost of reacting cleanly when the opponent moves first.

Stamina: 18

# **Universal Bukijutsu**
{:#universal-bukijutsu}

#### **Weapon Recovery**
{:#weapon-recovery}

A weapon on the ground is not “out of the fight”; it is a problem of distance, timing, and who reaches it first. You hook it with a foot, catch a returning cord, snatch it from the dirt, or draw a replacement without surrendering the exchange you already built.

Rank: E  
Stamina: 4  
Speed: 3  
Tags: Chain X, Utility, Bukijutsu  
Effects: Draw, pick up, or retrieve one weapon within arm’s reach without changing Chain value.

#### **Flowing Guard**
{:#flowing-guard}

A practiced stance change should not look like somebody stopping to remember a new pose. You let the old guard collapse naturally into the new one, moving hands, feet, and weapon through positions that remain dangerous the entire time.

Rank: D  
Stamina: 6  
Speed: 3  
Tags: Chain X, Utility, Bukijutsu  
Effects: Switch from one active Bukijutsu Stance to another Stance you know. Pay the new Stance’s activation cost.

#### **Weapon Breaker**
{:#weapon-breaker}

When an opponent trusts their weapon to catch yours, you punish the contact itself. The strike is allowed to bite into the defending edge, shaft, guard, or frame at the point of worst leverage, turning a successful defense into a durability problem.

Rank: B  
Stamina: \+10  
Speed: \+2  
Tags: Link, Bukijutsu  
Effects: Use retroactively after your weapon Attack is successfully Parried or Blocked by a weapon. After that Defense resolves, the defending weapon gains \+2 additional WDP.

#### **Chakra Reinforcement**
{:#chakra-reinforcement}

Chakra can make a weapon behave differently without changing what it physically is. You reinforce the part that matters for the injury you want—edge, point, striking face, or a projected chakra surface—letting one familiar weapon answer several kinds of protection.

Rank: B  
Chakra: 22  
Upkeep: 5  
Speed: 10  
Tags: Utility, Self, Upkeep, Bukijutsu  
Effects: Choose Blunt, Slashing, Piercing or Energy. One held weapon may deal that wound type and gains \+2 Damage Bonus.

#### **Chakra Reach**
{:#chakra-reach}

Shaped chakra extends the weapon’s edge, point, or striking line beyond its physical reach, carrying the threat several yards past the material weapon.

Rank: A  
Chakra: 20  
Upkeep: 3  
Speed: 10  
Tags: Utility, Self, Upkeep, Bukijutsu  
Effects: One Melee Weapon gains \+4 Reach and does not trigger ordinary on-contact punishment caused solely by touching the defender.

# **Sword Bukijutsu**
{:#sword-bukijutsu}

*Sword is the dueling weapon: it reads defensive habits, changes angle and punishes predictable answers*.

## **E-Rank**
{:#e-rank-10}

#### **Rising Cut**
{:#rising-cut}

The cut is meant to raise the opponent’s hands and eyes. A clean hit gives you the choice to press that lifted guard or take the outside angle before they can square up again.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+3  
Damage: \+2dS  
Speed: \-1  
Tags: Chain 1, Sword, Attack, Movement  
Effects: On a full hit, choose one: Guard Lift — note the Defense used against this Attack; if the target uses that same broad Defense against your next Sword Chain attack before 20 IC, that Defense takes \-2; or Take the Outside — after the Attack resolves, you may use any remaining Move with an Attack distance in any legal direction.

#### **Draw Cut**
{:#draw-cut}

The sheath is used as part of the attack rather than as storage. You can spend the draw on raw timing, or let the same motion carry you past the target instead of stopping in front of them.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+4  
Damage: \+1dS  
Speed: \+0  
Accuracy: \+1  
Tags: Chain 1, Sword, Attack, Movement  
Effects: If the sword began sheathed, choose one before rolling: Flash Draw — reduce this Attack’s final Speed by 2 after modifiers; or Passing Draw — do not gain that Speed reduction, but after a full hit you may use any remaining Move with an Attack distance in any legal direction. The sword ends drawn either way.

#### **Descending Cut**
{:#descending-cut}

This is the weight of the sequence coming down after the entry has already forced the guard upward. The user decides whether to cash that opening out as damage or smother the defender’s weapon line.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+5  
Damage: \+3dS  
Speed: \+1  
Tags: Chain 2, Sword, Attack  
Effects: If your previous Chain attack was Chain 1, choose one when this Attack fully hits: Drop Weight — treat this Attack as 4 Speed higher for damage only; or Smother the Guard — note the Defense used against this Attack; that same broad Defense takes \-2 against your next Sword Chain attack before 20 IC.

#### **Finishing Cut**
{:#finishing-cut}

This is where the sword chain is meant to end. The user commits the hips and shoulders to a cut chosen for wound placement, not simply for making another contact.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+8  
Damage: \+5dS  
Speed: \+3  
Accuracy: \-1  
Tags: Chain 3, Sword, Attack  
Effects: \+10% Wounding. You may make this a Called Shot; reduce that Called Shot penalty by 2\.

#### **Half-Step**
{:#half-step}

The step changes the user’s relationship to the target without putting the sword away or letting the chain go neutral. It is footwork for changing shoulders, hips, and escape lanes while the blade remains live.

Rank: E  
Stamina: 4  
Speed: 2  
Tags: Chain X, Sword, Utility, Movement  
Effects: Move up to 3 yards without breaking your Chain. You may move around the chained target while remaining within its Reach.

#### **Iaidō**
{:#iaido}

A draw-cut performed from stillness, relying on timing and a clean line rather than a long wind-up. The sheath, hips, and first step all become part of the same motion, so the technique is strongest when used before the sword has already been brought into a wide exchange.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+6  
Damage: \+3dS  
Speed: \-2  
Tags: Bukijutsu, Sword, Attack  
Effects: Requires a sheathed sword. On the first use against a creature this combat, its Defensive Interrupts count as \+2 Base Speed for Defense Validity. Ends drawn.

## **D-Rank**
{:#d-rank-10}

#### **Reversing Cut**
{:#reversing-cut}

The blade crosses the line the previous attack established, forcing the defender to decide whether to repeat a familiar answer or abandon it and give up position.

Rank: D  
Stamina: \+8  
Damage: \+3dS  
Speed: \+2  
Tags: Chain 2, Sword, Attack, Movement  
Effects: Read the Defense used against your previous Chain attack. If the target uses the same broad Defense again, that Defense takes \-2. If it changes to a different broad Defense instead, after this Attack resolves you may use any remaining Move with an Attack distance in any legal direction.

#### **Reverse Edge**
{:#reverse-edge}

The blade never truly resets after the previous exchange. A recovery that would normally bring the sword home is turned into a second cutting line, especially punishing a defense that only barely caught the first.

Rank: D  
Stamina: \+10  
Damage: \+5dS  
Speed: \-1  
Tags: Chain 3, Sword, Attack  
Effects: If the previous Chain attack was partially defended, treat the target's Defense as 1 point worse for this Attack.

#### **Bind and Recover**
{:#bind-and-recover}

The incoming weapon is received on the strong of the blade, then guided rather than simply stopped. Good contact leaves your own edge pointed somewhere useful for the answer.

Rank: D  
Stamina: 6  
Speed: 5  
Tags: Chain X, Sword, Utility, Interrupt  
Effects: Parry a Melee Attack with \+1. On a full success, your next Sword Chain attack against that attacker gains \+2 Accuracy.

#### **Tempered Edge**
{:#tempered-edge}

Chakra is run along the cutting geometry of the weapon, tightening the edge and keeping it from skidding or twisting on hard contact.

Rank: D  
Chakra: 12  
Upkeep: 2  
Speed: 7  
Tags: Bukijutsu, Sword, Utility  
Effects: One sword gains \+10% Wounding. This Wounding bonus does not stack with a Wounding bonus printed on a Sword Bukijutsu; use the higher value.

## **C-Rank**
{:#c-rank-10}

#### **Half-Sword Thrust**
{:#half-sword-thrust}

You shorten the sword and drive the point through a seam in the target’s protection.

Stamina: \+9  
Damage: \+5dS  
Speed: \+2  
Accuracy: \+1  
Tags: Chain 1, Sword, Attack  
Effects: Treat this Attack as Piercing. Reduce the target Armor’s Absorption by 10% for this Attack, to a minimum of 0%.

#### **Broken Rhythm**
{:#broken-rhythm}

The user sells one line with the eyes, shoulders, or early blade movement and attacks the reaction to it. The technique is strongest when the opponent believes they recognized the first intention.

Rank: C  
Stamina: 7  
Speed: 3  
Tags: Chain X, Sword, Utility  
Effects: Opposed Espionage vs Awareness against your chained target. On success, its next Defense against your Sword Chain attack before 20 IC takes \-2.

#### **Circular Draw**
{:#circular-draw}

The draw opens into a broad arc that claims space rather than chasing one perfect guard. The user decides how much of the motion is spent threatening several bodies and how much remains for recovery.

Damage: \+6dS  
Speed: \+4  
Line: 3, 1  
Tags: Bukijutsu, Sword, Attack  
Effects: Attack every chosen enemy in the line with one Accuracy roll; divide Speed×PDB evenly among targets hit.

## **B-Rank**
{:#b-rank-10}

#### **Intercepting Draw**
{:#intercepting-draw}

The draw and the defense are the same motion; the blade clears the sheath as it catches the incoming line.

#### **Draw Reset**
{:#draw-reset}

The sheath becomes a timing tool. A practiced resheath threatens another draw while preserving the rhythm of the current exchange instead of signalling that the fight has reset.

Speed: 3

Effects: Sheathe one sword you hold. Your next Sword Chain 1 before 20 IC has \-1 Speed.

## **A-Rank**

{:#final-measure}

You commit to the exact line the exchange has exposed, choosing whether to destroy the guard or carry the target through the cut.

If it succeeds, you may use your Move with an Attack movement to follow the target, ending adjacent if you can reach it.

#### **Perfect Bind**
{:#perfect-bind}

You catch the attacking weapon close to its strong and turn the opponent with the bind rather than simply stopping them.

On a full success, choose one: Turn the Line — make an Athletics Status Roll against the attacker’s Resistance; on success, move the attacker up to 3 yards to a legal space within your Reach; or Seal the Line — the attacking weapon takes \-3 Parry until the attacker’s next action.

# **Club Bukijutsu**
{:#club-bukijutsu}

*Club is the force weapon: it wins through impact, resisted Specials, armor pressure, collisions and terrain*.

## **E-Rank**
{:#e-rank-11}

#### **Driving Blow**
{:#driving-blow}

The blow is thrown through the target rather than at them. Its real payoff is deciding how far the impact carries the opponent and whether you chase into the space you just created.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+3  
Damage: \+2dS  
Speed: \+1  
Special: Knockback 3  
Tags: Chain 1, Club, Attack, Movement  
Effects: On a successful Special, you may follow the target up to half the distance it was actually moved as part of the Attack.

#### **Rising Blow**
{:#rising-blow}

The head of the weapon rises under the target’s posture, using shock to lift the chin, ribs, or guard. Against somebody already rattled, the same leverage can be spent on damage instead of more Stun.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+4  
Damage: \+2dS  
Speed: \+2  
Accuracy: \-1  
Special: Stun 3  
Tags: Chain 1, Club, Attack  
Effects: Apply Stun 3 normally. If the target was already Stunned before this Attack hit, do not apply this Stun and add \+2dS damage instead.

#### **Returning Swing**
{:#returning-swing}

This swing uses the opponent’s recent aggression as information. The user lets the counterweight and return path of the club erase awkward angles that would normally spoil Accuracy, then turns that recovered structure into force.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+5  
Damage: \+3dS  
Speed: \+2  
Tags: Chain 2, Club, Attack  
Effects: If the target attacked you in the last 20 IC, ignore up to 1 point of Accuracy penalties on this Attack. For each ignored point, gain \+1 Damage Bonus for this Attack.

#### **Overhead Blow**
{:#overhead-blow}

This is a deliberately obvious commitment from above. The user accepts the risk of a missed heavy swing in exchange for putting the club where a head-level impact can matter immediately.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+8  
Damage: \+5dS  
Speed: \+5  
Accuracy: \-2  
Special: Stun 5  
Tags: Chain 3, Club, Attack  
Effects: Must be a Called Shot to the Head. \+5% Wounding. On a full miss, your next Defense before your next action takes \-1.

#### **Brace**
{:#brace}

The club is braced with the body behind it, turning the weapon into a short piece of mobile cover. The point is to survive the collision without sacrificing the tool to durability damage.

Rank: E  
Stamina: 4  
Speed: 5  
Tags: Chain X, Club, Utility, Interrupt  
Effects: Block or Parry a Melee Attack with \+1. The first time each Round the club would gain WDP from that Block or Parry, reduce the WDP gained by 1, to a minimum of 0\.

#### **Wide Swing**
{:#wide-swing}

The weapon is allowed to travel through a wide arc and threaten several enemies at once. The user gives up some concentrated force in exchange for making nearby opponents respect the same swing.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+6  
Damage: \+2dS  
Speed: \+3  
Area: 2  
Special: Knockback 2  
Tags: Bukijutsu, Club, Attack  
Effects: Attack all enemies in reach. Divide Speed×PDB evenly among targets hit. Resolve the Special separately against each fully hit target.

## **D-Rank**
{:#d-rank-11}

#### **Rib Shot**
{:#rib-shot}

The strike is aimed where breathing, posture, and balance meet. Depending on what matters more, the user can make the target’s next attack clumsy or simply rattle their frame with Stun.

Rank: D  
Stamina: \+8  
Damage: \+4dS  
Speed: \+1  
Tags: Chain 2, Club, Attack  
Effects: On a full hit, choose one: Smother the Breath — the target takes \-2 Accuracy on its next Attack before 20 IC, increased to \-3 if it was already Stunned when this Attack hit; or Rattle the Frame — apply Stun 4\.

#### **Armor Crack**
{:#armor-crack}

Instead of slipping around armor or a barrier, the club attacks the thing protecting the target. The technique is about putting mass through structure until the defense itself becomes damaged terrain.

Rank: D  
Stamina: \+12  
Damage: \+5dS  
Speed: \+4  
Accuracy: \-1  
Tags: Chain 3, Club, Attack  
Effects: Before damaging the target, deal 65% of this Attack's final damage to one Armor piece, Barrier, or Construct directly between you and the target.

#### **Body Check**
{:#body-check}

The shoulder joins the weapon system. A body check can move an opponent without spending the club’s striking surface, preserving the weapon for the next beat while still changing distance.

Rank: D  
Stamina: 5  
Speed: 4  
Special: Knockback 3  
Tags: Chain X, Club, Utility, Movement  
Effects: If adjacent to your chained target, resolve the Knockback Special without dealing damage. On success you may move into the space the target left.

#### **Head-Heavy Grip**
{:#head-heavy-grip}

Grip and chakra bias the weapon toward the striking head, exaggerating leverage and reach. Each swing carries more of the weapon’s mass through the head, turning broad motion into crushing displacement.

Rank: D  
Chakra: 14  
Upkeep: 3  
Speed: 8  
Tags: Bukijutsu, Club, Utility  
Effects: One blunt weapon gains \+1 Reach. Whenever one of its Bukijutsu has a printed Knockback Special, increase that printed Knockback by 1 before rolling it. It may deal Blunt wounds even if another property would change its wound type.

## **C-Rank**
{:#c-rank-11}

#### **Low Sweep**
{:#low-sweep}

You drive the club through the target’s base instead of through their centre, forcing them to choose between footing and impact.

Damage: \+5dS

#### **Short Grip**
{:#short-grip}

A heavy weapon becomes dangerous to its owner when the grip gets too long, too short, or too awkward. This technique is the practiced reset that restores leverage without giving up the chain.

Speed: 2

Effects: Remove up to 3 points of temporary Accuracy penalty affecting your next Club Chain attack, or reduce its Delay by 2 (minimum 0).

#### **Wallbreaker**
{:#wallbreaker}

This is the answer to doors, walls, armor, and anything else that assumes being hard is the same as being safe. The technique is built to keep meaningful damage on the target even when ordinary hits would be absorbed.

#### **Guard Rattle**
{:#guard-rattle}

You strike through the defending line hard enough that the weapon or arm catching the blow becomes the problem.

# **Polearm Bukijutsu**
{:#polearm-bukijutsu}

*Polearm owns measure: Reach, approach denial, intercepts and repositioning are its main advantages*.

## **E-Rank**
{:#e-rank-12}

{:#passing-thrust}

The thrust is carried through a passing step so the point threatens beyond the weapon’s usual measure. The user can finish closer or farther away depending on where they want the next exchange to happen.

The hook, blade, or shaft catches posture instead of simply striking it. Once contact is made, the user chooses whether to turn the target into a new space or sacrifice damage to put them on the ground.

You brace the haft and drive the point through the target’s line with enough commitment that the impact itself disrupts their movement. The technique is dangerous because it gives up easy recovery in exchange for keeping the point exactly where the opponent least wants it.

# **Chain Weapon Bukijutsu**
{:#chain-weapon-bukijutsu}

*Chain weapons are the control weapon: their dedicated Bukijutsu turn the existing Grapple and Control system into a ranged weapon discipline*.

## **Weapon Grapples**
{:#weapon-grapples}

Chain Weapon Bukijutsu use the existing Grapple system rather than a separate restraint system. When a technique says it initiates a Weapon Grapple, the Chain weapon acts as the limbs maintaining the hold. The user is still the grappler for Grapple Offense, Grapple Defense, Control, Pressure, Grip Fighting and every other rule that modifies Grapples.

Initiating the Grapple: If a Chain Bukijutsu says it initiates a Weapon Grapple, resolve its weapon Attack first. On the required hit, the target enters a Clinch with the Chain weapon as though it had been hit by Grab, except the Grapple may be established at the Chain weapon's Range. Determine starting Control using the normal opposing Grapple checks. This is not a second attack or Defense.

Using Grapple Jutsu through the Chain: Chain Bukijutsu with Pummel, Trapping, Takedown, Ground Fighting, or Submission use those tags exactly as written in the normal Grapple rules. The Chain weapon supplies the limbs required to perform those techniques, so these Bukijutsu ignore the normal requirement that all four of the user's limbs be free. Ordinary Grapple jutsu do not gain range merely because you are holding a Chain weapon unless another rule says they do.

Remote Clinch: The target suffers the normal effects of a Clinch. The wielder does not suffer the Clinch's Dodge/Parry, movement, Ignite-transfer or Stealth restrictions solely because the Chain is holding the target at range. The wielder still loses Control normally for actions they take while maintaining the Grapple. The target may use Break Clinch and other legal escape effects against the wielder's normal Grapple values.

Weapon Pin: When a Chain Bukijutsu successfully uses Takedown, the target becomes Pinned by the Chain. The target suffers the normal effects of a Pin, including the normal escape rules, but the wielder does not become Prone and does not have their Speed reduced to 0 simply because the weapon is maintaining the Pin. Ground Fighting and Submission techniques delivered by Chain Bukijutsu function normally from this state.

Occupied Weapon: A Chain weapon maintaining a Weapon Grapple is occupied. It may only make attacks or use techniques that explicitly function during that Weapon Grapple. Releasing, losing, destroying, or using that same Chain weapon to establish a different Weapon Grapple ends the first.

Range and Movement: A Weapon Grapple does not cause the wielder and target to automatically move together as a normal Clinch does. The target cannot voluntarily end an Action farther from the wielder or current anchor than the Chain weapon's Range allows. If either participant is moved so the Chain can no longer reach, the Weapon Grapple ends unless a technique says otherwise.

You cross both attack lines through different parts of the target, forcing the defender to contain two wound lines at once before either hand can recover.

Stamina: \+12  
Damage: \+6dS  
Speed: \+6  
Accuracy: \-2  
Tags: Chain 3, Dual, Attack  
Effects: Requires two weapons. On a full hit, choose two different wound locations; if the Attack wounds, apply one wound at the normal location and a second wound one severity lower at the other location. The secondary wound cannot exceed Minor severity.

#### **Hand Feint**
{:#hand-feint}

Two visible threats are used to split attention before the attack starts. The user chooses which defensive habit—Dodge or Parry—they are trying to make less reliable on the next Chain action.

Rank: D  
Stamina: 5  
Speed: 3  
Tags: Chain X, Dual, Utility  
Effects: Opposed Espionage vs Awareness. On success, choose Dodge or Parry; that defense takes \-2 against your next Dual Chain attack.

#### **Paired Flow**
{:#paired-flow}

The weapons settle into a shared rhythm, one hand recovering while the other remains live. The alternating cadence makes both parries and follow-up attacks tighter and more economical.

Rank: D  
Stamina: 9  
Upkeep: 2  
Speed: 7  
Tags: Bukijutsu, Dual, Utility  
Effects: While wielding two weapons, gain \+1 Parry. Once per 20 IC, reduce the Speed of one Dual Chain attack by 2 after all other modifiers, minimum 3\.

## **C-Rank**
{:#c-rank-15}

#### **Split Pursuit**
{:#split-pursuit}

Each hand takes a different opponent, opening pressure on two fronts before the user decides which target to pursue.

Rank: C  
Stamina: \+8  
Speed: \+2  
Tags: Chain 1, Dual, Attack  
Effects: Make one Attack with each weapon at 60% final damage. The attacks must target different creatures. If either hits, choose one hit creature as your chained target.

#### **Changing Lead**
{:#changing-lead}

The style changes which weapon is “leading” without changing what is held. This lets the user decide which damage type and hand-specific penalties matter for the next few beats.

Rank: C  
Stamina: 5  
Speed: 1  
Tags: Chain X, Dual, Utility  
Effects: Swap which held weapon is leading. Until your next action, use either weapon's damage type for your Dual Chain effects and ignore one hand-specific penalty of up to 3\.

## **B-Rank**
{:#b-rank-15}

#### **Weapon Lock**
{:#weapon-lock}

The two weapons close around the line the defender committed, trapping that weapon between them for the next exchange.

Rank: B  
Stamina: \+14  
Damage: \+6dS  
Speed: \+3  
Accuracy: \-1  
Tags: Chain 2, Dual, Attack  
Effects: Requires two weapons. On a full hit, if the target used a weapon to Block or Parry, that weapon cannot be used to Block or Parry your next Dual Chain attack before 20 IC.

#### **Counterpair**
{:#counterpair}

A full parry with one hand opens the line for the other. Before the attacker can recover their weapon, the off-hand snaps through the space their attack just exposed.

Rank: B  
Stamina: 10  
Speed: 4  
Tags: Chain X, Dual, Attack, Interrupt  
Effects: After fully Parrying a Melee Attack, make an off-hand Basic Weapon Attack against that attacker at 65% final damage. This is Chain X.

## **A-Rank**
{:#a-rank-15}

#### **Crossed Execution**
{:#crossed-execution}

One hand attacks the target while the other deliberately destroys the guard protecting them.

Rank: A  
Stamina: \+26  
Speed: \+6  
Tags: Chain 3, Dual, Attack  
Effects: Requires two weapons. Make one Attack with each. One weapon attacks the target at 75% final damage; the other attacks one held weapon or Armor piece at 75% final damage. Roll and defend separately.

#### **Divergent Current**
{:#divergent-current}

Each weapon carries a different chakra treatment, letting the pair answer two different defenses in the same exchange.

Rank: A  
Chakra: 26  
Upkeep: 6  
Speed: 12  
Tags: Bukijutsu, Dual, Utility  
Effects: Apply two different weapon-coating effects you know, one to each held weapon. Pay this Upkeep plus only the higher additional Upkeep among those effects.

# **Bow Bukijutsu**
{:#bow-bukijutsu}

*Bow is the hunter weapon: marks, sightlines, prediction and movement make its best shots better than simply firing again*.

## **E-Rank**
{:#e-rank-16}

#### **Marking Shot**
{:#marking-shot}

The first arrow is used to establish a sightline rather than chase immediate damage. Once the mark is in place, the next Bow Chain 2 has a clearer reference for where the target is moving.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+3  
Damage: \+2dS  
Speed: \-1  
Accuracy: \+1  
Tags: Chain 1, Bow, Attack  
Effects: Bow only. On a full hit, apply your Bow Mark to the target for 20 IC. Your next Bow Chain 2 against that target gains \+2 Accuracy, increased to \+3 if the target moved at least 3 yards after being Marked. A new Bow Mark from you replaces the previous one.

#### **Pinning Shot**
{:#pinning-shot}

The arrow is aimed to catch cloth, gear, or flesh against the environment. The technique gets stronger near solid terrain because the shot has something physical to pin the target against.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+3  
Damage: \+1dS  
Speed: \+0  
Special: Immobilization 2  
Tags: Chain 1, Bow, Attack  
Effects: Bow only. Resolve the Immobilization Special normally. Against a target adjacent to solid terrain, increase the listed Immobilization by 1 before the Status Roll as the shot pins clothing or gear in place. The Immobilization lasts 10 IC.

#### **Angle Shot**
{:#angle-shot}

The release is chosen for a route the defender was not guarding—high, oblique, or around partial cover. It also tests whether the opponent keeps reaching for the same defensive answer.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+5  
Damage: \+3dS  
Speed: \+2  
Tags: Chain 2, Bow, Attack  
Effects: If the target uses the Cover Interrupt against this Attack, reduce Cover’s \+5 bonus by 2\. If the target uses the same broad Defense it used against your Chain 1, that Defense takes \-2.

#### **Multiple Shot**
{:#multiple-shot}

The bow is used as a platform for several distinct releases in one action. The user decides how many arrows matter and where they go, trading concentrated Speed×PDB for multiple wound checks that combine only when appropriate.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+8  
Speed: \+5  
Tags: Chain 3, Bow, Attack  
Effects: Fire 2 arrows. Make separate Accuracy rolls; divide Speed×PDB evenly among them. Each hit adds \+2dS. Combine hits for Wounding only.

#### **Quick Nock**
{:#quick-nock}

You nock the next arrow in the same motion that lowers the bow, keeping the string, grip, and sightline ready before the target sees a reset.

Rank: E  
Stamina: 3  
Speed: 2  
Tags: Chain X, Bow, Utility  
Effects: Nock an arrow. Your next Bow Chain attack before 20 IC has \-2 Speed, minimum Base Speed 3\.

#### **Volley**
{:#volley}

A high arc of arrows claims an area instead of one target, filling the space with descending shots and forcing everyone beneath it to find an answer at once.

Rank: E  
Stamina: 7  
Speed: 10  
Area: 4 at a point within Bow Range  
Tags: Bukijutsu, Bow, Attack  
Effects: Fire a volley; attack all chosen creatures in Area. Each target takes weapon damage with only 50% of Speed×PDB.

## **D-Rank**
{:#d-rank-16}

#### **Twin Arrow**
{:#twin-arrow}

Two arrows share one draw and release timing but remain separate attacks. The target has to solve both trajectories while the user accepts that each arrow carries less of the total force.

Stamina: \+8  
Speed: \+4  
Tags: Chain 2, Bow, Attack  
Effects: Fire two arrows at the same target, each at 60% final damage. Roll separately; Chain advances if either hits. The defender pays one Defensive cost but rolls separately.

#### **Precision Shot**
{:#precision-shot}

The final arrow is chosen for penetration and wound placement. A previous mark can be deliberately spent to turn the finisher into a more accurate shot instead of leaving the setup unused.

Rank: D  
Stamina: \+11  
Damage: \+6dS  
Speed: \+5  
Accuracy: \-2  
Tags: Chain 3, Bow, Attack  
Effects: \+10% Wounding. Reduce the target Armor’s Absorption by 10% for this Attack, to a minimum of 0%. If the target has your Bow Mark, you may consume it when declaring this Attack to gain \+3 Accuracy.

#### **Cutting Arrow**
{:#cutting-arrow}

An arrow is fired at another projectile rather than the attacker. It is an expensive kind of parry because the ammunition is spent either way, but it lets a bow answer threats most ranged weapons cannot.

Rank: D  
Stamina: 6  
Speed: 5  
Tags: Chain X, Bow, Utility, Interrupt  
Effects: Fire at an incoming Ranged Taijutsu projectile and Parry it with \+1. The arrow used is consumed whether you succeed or fail.

#### **Held Draw**
{:#held-draw}

The bow is held in a disciplined firing posture, the sightline settling more firmly each moment the target remains predictable. Large movements force the user to rebuild that alignment.

Rank: D  
Stamina: 9  
Upkeep: 2  
Speed: 7  
Tags: Bukijutsu, Bow, Utility  
Effects: While maintained, Bow attacks gain \+2 Accuracy against targets that have not moved since your last action. If you move for any reason, you lose this bonus until your next action.

## **C-Rank**
{:#c-rank-16}

{:#threaded-shot}

You place the arrow directly through one target’s line and keep enough force behind it to threaten the body standing behind them.

Damage: \+4dS

{:#reinforced-arrow}

Chakra is fed into the ammunition as it leaves the bow, hardening the arrow’s flight and edge so it can bite cleanly into both flesh and chakra-made structures.

Effects: Ammunition fired from one bow counts as chakra-enhanced, gains \+10% Wounding, and may damage chakra constructs normally.

## **B-Rank**
{:#b-rank-16}

#### **Anchor Shot**
{:#anchor-shot}

The arrow is driven through gear or flesh and fixed into nearby terrain, turning a hit into a hard limit on where the target can go.

Stamina: \+14  
Damage: \+6dS  
Speed: \+4  
Accuracy: \-1  
Tags: Chain 2, Bow, Attack

#### **Sightline**
{:#sightline}

The user spends a moment reading sightline, cover, air, and target movement before firing. The payoff is temporary certainty: better information turns into ignored Visibility or Cover penalties and a longer effective range.

Stamina: 9  
Speed: 4

{:#a-rank-16}

# **Thrown Bukijutsu**
{:#thrown-bukijutsu}

*Thrown weapons are the toolbox: ricochets, wire, multiple targets and prepared gear reward adapting to the battlefield*.

## **E-Rank**
{:#e-rank-17}

#### **Double Cast**
{:#double-cast}

Two compatible tools leave the hand on nearly the same line. A full hit rewards the double release, while a Partial Success still catches only one weapon cleanly and can establish the chain.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+3  
Speed: \+2  
Tags: Chain 1, Thrown, Attack  
Effects: Throw two compatible weapons on one line. One Accuracy roll; on a full hit both land for 55% damage each. On a partial hit only one lands for full damage. Chain 1 is established if either hits.

#### **Needle Lead**
{:#needle-lead}

The first small projectile is placed where a minor injury can matter tactically. The user chooses whether the hit should bleed or simply spoil the target’s next attack.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+3  
Damage: \+1dS  
Speed: \-1  
Accuracy: \+1  
Tags: Chain 1, Thrown, Attack  
Effects: Requires Kunai, Shuriken or Senbon. On a full hit choose one: Bleed — make the normal Athletics Status Roll for Bleed 2; or Disrupt — the target takes \-2 Accuracy on its next Attack before 20 IC.

#### **Manipulated Shuriken**
{:#manipulated-shuriken}

Wire keeps a missed weapon relevant. Instead of accepting the first failed trajectory, the user spends the committed line to make the target answer a second, weaker approach.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+5  
Speed: \+3  
Tags: Chain 2, Thrown, Attack  
Effects: Requires Ninja Wire equal to Range. If your thrown Attack is successfully defended against, force the target to repeat that same Defense once at no additional Stamina, Chakra or AP cost. If the repeated Defense fails, the redirected Attack hits, but treat its final Speed as half as high for damage only. The wire is committed until recovered.

#### **Fuuma Barrage**
{:#fuuma-barrage}

Fuuma shuriken are committed in sequence rather than as one oversized hit. Repeated misses help the user walk the line onto the target, while each successful heavy weapon contributes its own share of the finisher.

Rank: E  
Stamina: \+8  
Speed: \+6  
Tags: Chain 3, Thrown, Attack  
Effects: Fuuma Shuriken only. Throw 2 weapons in succession; one Accuracy roll starts at \-2 and improves by \+1 for each previous miss. Each hit deals an equal share of the attack’s base weapon dice plus 2dS. Divide Speed×PDB evenly among the two weapons.

#### **Hidden Mouth Needle**
{:#hidden-mouth-needle}

A concealed senbon is produced without the ordinary draw that warns people a thrown attack is coming. The motion hides in breath, speech, and head movement until the needle is already in flight.

Rank: E  
Chakra: 6  
Speed: 3

Effects: Produce and spit one concealed Senbon without drawing ammunition. You may include it in a Senbon Multi-Throw if you can normally multi-throw Senbon. The first time in a battle you use Hidden Mouth Needle against a creature that has not seen you use it, that Attack is treated as a Surprise Attack even if you were not hidden.

#### **Field Volley**
{:#field-volley}

The throw widens into a spread of tools that blankets an area, the user fanning their release so several targets are threatened by the same handful.

Rank: E  
Stamina: 6  
Speed: 9  
Area: 4 within normal thrown Range

Effects: Make one multi-throw Accuracy roll against every chosen creature in Area. Each target can be hit by at most 1 weapon.

## **D-Rank**
{:#d-rank-17}

{:#banked-throw}

The throw is aimed at hard terrain on purpose. A banked angle can ignore some of the defense granted by cover and lets the user choose where the weapon finishes for later retrieval.

Speed: \+2

#### **Handful Throw**
{:#handful-throw}

The user empties a full handful in one practiced release, splitting the weapons between targets as the throw opens across the battlefield.

Rank: D  
Stamina: \+12  
Speed: \+6  
Tags: Chain 3, Thrown, Attack

#### **Return Flick**
{:#return-flick}

A missed or deflected weapon is treated as recoverable equipment rather than lost ammunition. The user reads the rebound or wire line and pulls the tool back before the fight moves on.

Stamina: 4  
Speed: 3  
Tags: Chain X, Thrown, Utility, Interrupt  
Effects: After a thrown weapon misses or is deflected, retrieve one weapon within 10 yards if a plausible line exists.

#### **Throwing Form**
{:#throwing-form}

Years of throwing have stripped wasted motion from the draw, grip, and release. Several tools can be indexed between the fingers and launched without the hands tangling or the stance collapsing.

Rank: D  
Stamina: 9  
Upkeep: 2  
Speed: 7  
Tags: Bukijutsu, Thrown, Utility  
Effects: Reduce the Speed added by multi-throw by 1 and increase your multi-throw limit by 2\. This cannot reduce a multi-throw below total Speed 5 or below any other normal system minimum.

## **C-Rank**
{:#c-rank-17}

#### **Crossfire Cast**
{:#crossfire-cast}

You split the throw across two opponents, using separate lines so neither target can read the whole release from one hand motion.

Rank: C  
Stamina: \+8  
Speed: \+2  
Tags: Chain 1, Thrown, Attack  
Effects: Throw one compatible weapon at each of two different creatures at 60% final damage. Roll separately. If either hits, choose one hit creature as your chained target.

#### **Corner Wire**
{:#corner-wire}

Ninja Wire is attached or routed before the next throw so the projectile can travel farther and turn a corner the hand alone could not produce. The line must still make physical sense on the battlefield.

Rank: C  
Stamina: 7  
Speed: 3

Effects: Attach or manipulate Ninja Wire. Your next Thrown Chain attack gains \+15 Range and may turn one corner of up to 90 degrees if line can be physically routed.

#### **Rigged Weapon**
{:#rigged-weapon}

Tags, wire loops, poisons, and other legal attachments are prepared before the throw instead of awkwardly during it.

Rank: C  
Stamina: 11  
Speed: 8  
Tags: Bukijutsu, Thrown, Utility  
Effects: Prepare one thrown weapon with up to two compatible attachments such as a paper tag, wire loop, poison applicator, or similar legal tool without increasing the Speed of the next throw. All normal item costs and restrictions still apply.

## **B-Rank**
{:#b-rank-17}

#### **Returning Arc**
{:#returning-arc}

The weapon continues through its first line and curves toward a second target before it loses momentum.

Rank: B  
Stamina: \+14  
Damage: \+7dS  
Speed: \+3  
Tags: Chain 2, Thrown, Attack  
Effects: After resolving this Attack, choose a second creature within 5 yards of the first target. Make a second Accuracy roll at \-1; on hit, deal 65% final damage. The second hit cannot establish or advance Chain.

#### **Scatter Run**
{:#scatter-run}

The user moves while preparing the next handful, drawing and indexing tools between steps so the next release is already assembled when the new angle opens.

Rank: B  
Stamina: 9

## **A-Rank**
{:#a-rank-17}

#### **Fuuma Reaper**
{:#fuuma-reaper}

You send a Fuuma Shuriken through a continuous cutting route, forcing several targets to solve the same spinning weapon from different angles.

Rank: A  
Stamina: \+26  
Speed: \+8
